The `:` separator is used from the start.\n- A task is **one folder** containing at minimum: `default.ts` (entrypoint), `README.md`, `settings.json` (task settings), and optionally `schema.json` (input schema) and `agents/<runtime>.json` (runtime descriptors).\n- A **task package** is a Bun package whose `tasks/` directory holds one subfolder per task. Shared code lives at the package root (or `lib/`) and tasks import it via relative paths.\n- Discovery is `$PATH`-like: the daemon walks search paths, reads each task folder's manifest, and builds an index mapping `namespace:id` → folder location + manifest.\n- Identity (namespace + id) is **declared in the task's manifest**, not inferred from the folder name.\n\n### 3.3 Input / context convention\n\n- Each task declares its input shape via `schema.json` (JSON Schema draft-07) referenced from `settings.json` (or inlined).\n- At `tq add <namespace:id> <context>`, the CLI sends the context; the registry parses + validates it against the schema before enqueueing.\n- The task's `default` receives the **parsed, validated input object**.\n- CLI convenience: when the schema has exactly one required string property, `tq add <id> <bare-string>` auto-wraps to `{\"<prop>\": \"<bare-string>\"}`. The authoritative input is always JSON validated against the schema.\n- Existing sample tasks (`ls`, `minfo`) declare `{ type: \"object\", properties: { filepath: { type: \"string\" } }, required: [\"filepath\"] }` and are used as `tq add default:ls '{\"filepath\":\"fixture/1\"}'` (or the convenience form `tq add default:ls fixture/1`).\n\n### 3.4 Execution contract\n\n- Runner invokes: `default(ctx: ExecutionContext) → Promise<void>`.\n- `ExecutionContext` provides: parsed input, `AbortSignal` (for `abort`), a stdout/stderr capture mechanism (streams or log callbacks), and a completion signal (callback or thrown error).\n- Runner owns **observability**: captures stdout/stderr, measures duration, transitions state. Task owns **execution**.\n- One `default` invocation = one task instance = one lifecycle. \"Task implements its own scheduling\" means the task logic may internally schedule sub-work, but from the runner's view it is one atomic instance.\n- Exit mapping: runner treats clean completion with exit code 0 as SUCCESS; non-zero exit or thrown error as FAILED. `abort` cancels the coroutine (and any child process the task spawned via `$`), preserves partial logs, and resets the instance to PENDING for retry.\n\n### 3.5 Queue model\n\n- One queue per task definition. Queue ID == task identity.\n- Per-queue concurrency configured in `settings.json` (e.g. `\"concurrency\": 3`).\n- Queue states: IDLE ⇄ PROCESSING ⇄ STOPPED.\n  - IDLE: queue exists, no RUNNING tasks, not actively draining (empty or freshly created).\n  - PROCESSING: actively draining PENDING tasks up to concurrency.\n  - STOPPED: finished current RUNNING tasks, will not start next PENDING.\n- `stop`: complete current RUNNING, do not start next PENDING → PROCESSING → STOPPED.\n- `start`: resume → STOPPED → PROCESSING (PENDING tasks remain and are picked up in order).\n- `abort`: kill current RUNNING, reset to PENDING, do not start next PENDING.\n- Drain order: FIFO / insertion order.\n\n### 3.6 Persistence\n\n- SQLite via `bun:sqlite`. DB holds: task definition registry, task instances (state, input, exitCode, timing, error), queue state, concurrency, running count.\n- Logs (stdout/stderr) are **files on disk** in `logs/` (already in `.gitignore`), referenced by path from the DB. Reasoning: logs can be large; SQLite is for structured lookups/dashboard queries.\n- DB location: configurable, default `./taskrunner.db` in the daemon's working directory (per-project DB); override via `--db` or `TASKRUNNER_DB`.\n\n### 3.7 Autostart\n\n- \"Autostart\" = on `tq daemon`, all registered queues automatically enter PROCESSING and begin draining PENDING tasks.\n- No systemd/reboot autostart in scope.\n- `tq add` writes to the DB directly, so tasks can be enqueued before the daemon runs; they are picked up on `tq daemon` or `tq start`.\n\n### 3.8 Dashboard\n\n- Web UI served by the daemon's HTTP server (`Bun.serve`), minimal HTML at `/`.\n- Real-time updates via SSE (state changes + log tailing).\n- REST API on the same server:\n  - `GET /` — dashboard HTML\n  - `GET /api/queues` — list queues\n  - `GET /api/queues/:id` — queue detail (state, concurrency, running count, task count)\n  - `GET /api/queues/:id/tasks` — tasks in queue (with state)\n  - `GET /api/tasks/:id` — task instance detail\n  - `GET /api/tasks/:id/logs` — log file content\n  - `GET /api/tasks/:id/logs/stream` — SSE log stream\n  - `POST /api/queues/:id/start` — start queue\n  - `POST /api/queues/:id/stop` — stop queue\n  - `POST /api/queues/:id/abort` — abort current instance\n  - `POST /api/tasks` — enqueue task (API equivalent of `tq add`)\n\n### 3.9 CLI (`tq`)\n\n- Same binary, two modes:\n  - `tq daemon` — start HTTP server + runner loop.\n  - `tq <subcommand>` — add, stop, start, abort, clear, status, logs, install, reload.\n- `tq` talks to a running daemon over HTTP when available; degrades to direct SQLite/DB access when the daemon is not running.\n- `tq add` writes to the DB directly (works without the daemon).\n- Sample workflow (from the prompt, adapted to namespace:id):\n\n```sh\ntq daemon          # starts service; registered tasks autostart\n\ntq add default:ls fixture/1\ntq add default:ls fixture/2\ntq add default:minfo fixture/1\ntq add default:minfo fixture/2\n\ntq stop default:ls\ntq start default:ls\ntq abort default:minfo\n\ntq clear default:ls        # clear SUCCESS tasks\ntq clear --all default:ls  # clear all tasks\n```\n\n---\n\n## 4. File and directory layout\n\n### 4.1 Project layout (this repo)\n\n```\n.\n├── index.ts            # daemon entrypoint (TBD)\n├── package.json\n├── bun.lock\n├── tsconfig.json\n├── SPEC.md             # this file\n├── tasks/              # project task search path (default namespace)\n│   ├── ls/\n│   │   ├── default.ts\n│   │   ├── README.md\n│   │   ├── settings.json\n│   │   └── schema.json\n│   └── minfo/\n│       ├── default.ts\n│       ├── README.md\n│       ├── settings.json\n│       └── schema.json\n├── fixture/\n│   ├── 1/1.srt\n│   └── 2/2.srt\n├── logs/               # task instance logs (gitignored)\n└── taskrunner.db       # SQLite DB (gitignored, configurable)\n```\n\n### 4.2 Task folder contents\n\nA task folder (e.g. `tasks/minfo/`) contains:\n\n| File | Purpose |\n|---|---|\n| `default.ts` | Entrypoint: `export default async (ctx: ExecutionContext) => void` |\n| `README.md` | Human documentation for the task |\n| `settings.json` | Task settings: concurrency, env, inputSchema ref, schedule ref, metadata |\n| `schema.json` | *(optional)* JSON Schema for the input; if absent, input is an opaque string |\n| `agents/<runtime>.json` | *(optional)* per-runtime descriptor (daemon, cli, …) |\n\nA task **package** root (e.g. an installable bun package) additionally has:\n\n```\nmy-package/\n├── package.json          # bun package: name, version, dependencies, \"tasks\": [\"ls\",\"minfo\"]\n├── lib/                  # shared code imported by tasks via relative paths\n│   └── media.ts\n└── tasks/\n    ├── ls/\n    └── minfo/\n```\n\n### 4.3 Lockfile\n\nA lockfile (e.g. `.taskrunner.lock` or `taskrunner-lock.json`) maps `namespace:id` → `{ source, installedPath, manifestHash }`, analogous to agent-skills' `skills-lock.json`. The daemon reads it to know installed-package task locations.\n\n---\n\n## 5. Task manifest and schema — illustrative shape\n\n### 5.1 `settings.json` (task settings)\n\n```json\n{\n  \"namespace\": \"default\",\n  \"id\": \"minfo\",\n  \"name\": \"Media info\",\n  \"description\": \"Print media information for a video/audio file\",\n  \"concurrency\": 1,\n  \"inputSchema\": \"schema.json\",\n  \"env\": {},\n  \"agents\": {\n    \"daemon\": { \"logPath\": \"logs\" },\n    \"cli\": { \"argumentHint\": \"<filepath>\", \"example\": \"tq add default:minfo fixture/1\" }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n### 5.2 `schema.json` (input schema)\n\n```json\n{\n  \"$schema\": \"http://json-schema.org/draft-07/schema#\",\n  \"type\": \"object\",\n  \"properties\": {\n    \"filepath\": { \"type\": \"string\" }\n  },\n  \"required\": [\"filepath\"]\n}\n```\n\n### 5.3 `agents/cli.json` (CLI runtime descriptor)\n\n```json\n{\n  \"display_name\": \"Media info\",\n  \"short_description\": \"Print media information for a video/audio file\",\n  \"argument_hint\": \"<filepath>\",\n  \"example\": \"tq add default:minfo fixture/1\"\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## 6. ExecutionContext interface (TypeScript shape)\n\n```ts\ninterface ExecutionContext<TInput> {\n  input: TInput;\n  signal: AbortSignal;\n  stdout: WritableStream | LogSink;\n  stderr: WritableStream | LogSink;\n  done: (exitCode: number) => void;\n}\n```\n\nThe runner wraps `default(ctx)` and, regardless of outcome, transitions the instance state and captures logs/timing. The task calls `ctx.done(0)` on success or throws / calls `ctx.done(nonZero)` on failure.\n\n---\n\n## 7. State machine summary\n\n### 7.1 Task instance states\n\nPENDING → RUNNING → SUCCESS\nPENDING → RUNNING → FAILED\nRUNNING → (abort) → PENDING (retry, partial logs preserved)\n\n### 7.2 Queue states\n\nIDLE → PROCESSING (when a task is launched and queue becomes active)\nPROCESSING → STOPPED (on `stop`)\nSTOPPED → PROCESSING (on `start`)\nPROCESSING → IDLE (when queue empties and no RUNNING tasks remain)\n\n---\n\n## 8. CLI command reference (settled)\n\n| Command | Meaning |\n|---|---|\n| `tq daemon` | Start the daemon (HTTP server + runner loop); queues autostart |\n| `tq add <namespace:id> <context>` | Enqueue a task instance (writes DB directly) |\n| `tq stop <namespace:id>` | Stop queue: finish RUNNING, don't start next PENDING |\n| `tq start <namespace:id>` | Resume queue: start processing PENDING |\n| `tq abort <namespace:id>` | Kill current RUNNING, reset to PENDING, don't start next |\n| `tq clear <namespace:id>` | Delete SUCCESS instances (+ logs) for the queue |\n| `tq clear --all <namespace:id>` | Delete all instances (except RUNNING) + logs |\n| `tq status` | Show queue/instance state (from DB or daemon API) |\n| `tq logs <instance-id>` | Show task instance logs |\n| `tq install <package>` | Install a task package, extract tasks/, update lockfile |\n| `tq reload` | Reload task discovery from search paths (SIGHUP alternative) |\n\n---\n\n## 9. Open issues / further discussion\n\nThese are still to clarify or implement; listed here as requested (\"log issues we would further discuss and clarify\").\n\n### 9.1 Input schema — inference vs. explicit\n\n- **Issue**: We chose explicit `schema.json` over inferring the input shape from the TS signature of `default`. Confirm this is right, and decide whether to support a *minimal* task (no schema → input is a plain string) as a first-class convenience, or always require a schema.\n- **Impact**: affects `tq add` parsing logic and how bare-string convenience wrapping works.\n\n### 9.2 `ExecutionContext` capture mechanism\n\n- **Issue**: The `stdout`/`stderr` capture in `ExecutionContext` is underspecified: is it a `WritableStream` the task writes to, a `log(str, isErr)` callback the task calls, or does the runner intercept `console.log`/`console.error` globally for the task's execution? Each has different ergonomics for tasks that use `console.log` (like the current `minfo.ts`).\n- **Impact**: affects the task entrypoint contract and how existing sample tasks are adapted.\n\n### 9.3 Same-process isolation guarantees\n\n- **Issue**: We chose same-process task execution with trusted tasks. Need to define what happens on: unhandled error (runner catches → FAILED), infinite loop (instance hangs → abort required), memory leak (no isolation → affects daemon). Decide whether to document these as \"trusted tasks\" caveats now, or add a lightweight per-instance timeout as a safety net.\n- **Impact**: affects robustness posture and whether `tq abort` needs a per-instance timeout.\n\n### 9.4 `tq` degradation behavior\n\n- **Issue**: `tq` talks HTTP to the daemon when available, direct DB otherwise. Open: how does `tq` detect the daemon is running? (TODO: check a PID file, a lock file, or try the API and fall back.) And does `tq add` via direct DB require the daemon to be notified (e.g. a wake-up signal) or is polling on the daemon side fine?\n- **Impact**: affects CLI implementation and daemon wake-up semantics.\n\n### 9.5 Log retention and clear semantics\n\n- **Issue**: `clear` is destructive (delete instance rows + log files). Open: should there be a retention policy (e.g. auto-clear SUCCESS older than N days), a non-destructive archive (`tq archive`), or per-instance log rotation? Also: can `clear --all` remove RUNNING instances? (Currently: no — refuses/aborts RUNNING.)\n- **Impact**: affects `tq clear` behavior and log lifecycle.\n\n### 9.6 Dashboard — command exposure\n\n- **Issue**: The dashboard is read-only + SSE; commands go through CLI or REST API. Open: do we want in-dashboard controls (start/stop/abort buttons) that call the REST API from the browser, or keep the dashboard strictly observational and push all ops to the CLI?\n- **Impact**: affects dashboard UI scope.\n\n### 9.7 Task package install mechanics\n\n- **Issue**: `tq install <package>` is sketched as a convenience (bun install + extract `tasks/` + update lockfile). Open: where do installed package tasks land — `node_modules/<pkg>/tasks/` (discovered via the lockfile) or an extracted copy in `~/.taskrunner/tasks/<pkg>/`? And how are package dependencies of a task (e.g. a task that `import`s a third-party lib) resolved — does the daemon need the package installed, or are tasks bundled?\n- **Impact**: affects install path, lockfile semantics, and module resolution for tasks.\n\n### 9.8 Namespace shadowing and search-path order\n\n- **Issue**: Search paths are walked in order (project `tasks/`, user `~/.taskrunner/tasks/`, installed-package locations). Open: is the first match the winner ( shadowing ), or do we merge/warn on conflicts? And can a user task in `~/.taskrunner/tasks/` shadow a project task with the same `namespace:id`?\n- **Impact**: affects discovery semantics and conflict behavior.\n\n### 9.9 Daemon configuration\n\n- **Issue**: Configuration is underspecified: DB path, HTTP port, search paths, log directory, concurrency defaults. Open: is there a config file (`taskrunner.toml` / `taskrunner.json` / `.taskrunnerrc`), env vars, CLI flags, or a mix? And where does it live (project-level, user-level)?\n- **Impact**: affects startup and portability.\n\n### 9.10 `tq reload` and live task discovery\n\n- **Issue**: We have `tq reload` (re-walk search paths, e.g. on SIGHUP). Open: does reload also re-read task manifests/schemas (so a changed `settings.json` takes effect without restart), or is reload only for newly added/removed task folders?\n- **Impact**: affects operational model for updating tasks.\n\n### 9.11 Sample tasks adaptation\n\n- **Issue**: The existing `tasks/ls.ts` and `tasks/minfo.ts` export `default (filepath: string) => Promise<number>`. They need to be adapted to the new `ExecutionContext` contract and reformatted as task folders (with `README.md`, `settings.json`, `schema.json`). Open: do we keep the current `.ts` files as the `default.ts` body (wrapped) or rewrite them?\n- **Impact**: affects the first batch of implemented tasks and whether the samples still work as before.\n\n---\n\n## 10. Out of scope (for now)\n\n- Systemd/reboot autostart of the daemon.\n- Per-instance process isolation (child-process sandboxing).\n- Priority queues, explicit priorities, weighted scheduling.\n- Non-destructive archive / retention policies (defer to `tq archive` + retention later).\n- WebSocket bidirectional dashboard (SSE is enough for now).\n- Multiple concurrent runtimes/agents per task beyond the daemon + CLI descriptors.\n- Service tasks / long-running persistent workers (one instance = one `default` invocation for now).\n"
}
````

Unclear what happens on unhandled error (runner catches → FAILED — probably fine), infinite loop (instance hangs → abort required), and memory leak (no isolation → affects daemon).\n- **Impact**: robustness posture; whether `tq abort` needs a per-instance timeout; whether we document \"trusted tasks\" caveats now.\n- **Suggested next step**: Add a per-instance timeout (configurable, default e.g. 30 min) as a safety net, and document the trusted-tasks caveat. Unclear whether to add auto-clear of old SUCCESS tasks, a non-destructive `tq archive`, or per-instance log rotation. Also whether `clear --all` can remove RUNNING (currently: no).\n- **Impact**: `tq clear` behavior; log lifecycle; disk usage over time.\n- **Suggested next step**: Keep `clear` destructive for now, refuse RUNNING on `--all`, and defer archive/retention to a later `tq archive` command.\n\n## Dashboard — command exposure\n\n- **What's unsettled**: Whether the dashboard gets in-page start/stop/abort buttons (calling the REST API from the browser) or stays observational with all ops on the CLI.\n- **Impact**: dashboard UI scope; whether the REST API endpoints for start/stop/abort need auth/rate-limiting (they don't if only CLI uses them, but do if the dashboard calls them).\n- **Suggested next step**: Start observational; add in-dashboard controls later if wanted. REST API for start/stop/abort is still useful as the CLI's backend and for future tooling.\n\n## Task package install mechanics\n\n- **What's unsettled**: Where installed package tasks land — `node_modules/<pkg>/tasks/` discovered via lockfile, or an extracted copy in `~/.taskrunner/tasks/<pkg>/`. Also how a task's own npm dependencies are resolved (does the daemon need the package installed, or are tasks bundled).\n- **Impact**: install path; lockfile semantics; module resolution for tasks; whether `tq install` is just a bun add + symlink or a real extraction.\n- **Suggested next step**: Start with `node_modules/<pkg>/tasks/` discovered via lockfile (no extraction; bun resolution handles deps). Add extraction to a user dir later if isolation/isolation-from-project is wanted.\n\n## Namespace shadowing and search-path order\n\n- **What's unsettled**: Search paths walked in order (project `tasks/`, user `~/.taskrunner/tasks/`, installed-package locations). Unclear if first match wins (shadowing) or if we merge/warn on conflicts, and whether a user task can shadow a project task with the same `namespace:id`.\n- **Impact**: discovery semantics; conflict behavior; predictability for users.\n- **Suggested next step**: First match wins (project can't shadow user by default? or project wins over user?). Decide order and shadowing rules, then warn on conflicts at `tq reload`.\n\n## Daemon configuration\n\n- **What's unsettled**: How config is expressed (config file `taskrunner.toml`/`taskrunner.json`/`.taskrunnerrc`, env vars, CLI flags, or a mix), and where it lives (project-level, user-level).\n- **Impact**: startup behavior; portability; per-project vs. global daemon behavior.\n- **Suggested next step**: Project-level config file (`taskrunner.toml`) for DB path, HTTP port, search paths, log dir, concurrency defaults; CLI flags override; env vars as a fallback. No user-level config for now.\n\n## `tq reload` and live task discovery\n\n- **What's unsettled**: Whether `tq reload` re-reads task manifests/schemas (so a changed `settings.json` takes effect without restart) or only picks up newly added/removed task folders.\n- **Impact**: operational model for updating tasks; whether queue concurrency changes take effect on reload.\n- **Suggested next step**: `tq reload` re-reads manifests/schemas for existing task folders AND picks up new/removed folders; concurrency changes take effect on reload (already-running instances are unaffected, new launches use the new limit).\n\n## Sample tasks adaptation\n\n- **What's unsettled**: The existing `tasks/ls.ts` and `tasks/minfo.ts` export `default (filepath: string) => Promise<number>`. {
  "path": "tasks/ls.ts",
  "replacements": [
    {
      "oldString": "import { $ } from \"bun\";\n\nexport default async (filepath: string) => {\n  const { stdout, stderr, exitCode } = await $`ls \"${filepath}\"`\n    .nothrow()\n    .quiet();\n  return 0;\n};\n",
      "newString": "import { $ } from \"bun\";\n\nexport default async function ls(filepath: string) {\n  const { stdout, stderr, exitCode } = await $`ls \"${filepath}\"`\n    .nothrow()\n    .quiet();\n  if (stdout) console.log(stdout.toString());\n  if (stderr) console.error(stderr.toString());\n  return exitCode === null ? 0 : exitCode;\n}\n\n/**\n * @param ctx - ExecutionContext for the task runner.\n * Wraps the plain `ls(filepath)` function so the runner can capture logs\n * and drive state transitions.\n */\nexport async function runWith(ctx: {\n  input: { filepath: string };\n  signal: AbortSignal;\n  done: (exitCode: number) => void;\n}) {\n  try {\n    if (ctx.signal.aborted) {\n      ctx.done(124);\n      return;\n    }\n    const exitCode = await ls(ctx.input.filepath);\n    ctx.done(exitCode);\n  } catch (err) {\n    console.error(err);\n    ctx.done(1);\n  }\n}\n",
      "allowMultiple": false
    }
  ]
}
```

```

- path: tasks/minfo.ts
  content: |
      import { $ } from "bun";
      
      function padZero(num: number, size: number): string {
        let s = num.toString();
        while (s.length < size) {
          s = "0" + s;
        }
        return s;
      }
      
      export function prettyTime(seconds: number): string {
        const hours = Math.floor(seconds / 3600);
        const minutes = Math.floor((seconds % 3600) / 60);
        const secs = Math.floor(seconds % 60);
      
        return `${padZero(hours, 2)}:${padZero(minutes, 2)}:${padZero(secs, 2)}`;
      }
      
      export function prettyBps(bps: string | number): string {
        let bytesN = 0;
        if (typeof bps == "string") {
          bytesN = parseInt(bps, 10);
        } else {
          bytesN = bps;
        }
        if (bytesN === 0) return "0 B";
        const k = 1024;
        const sizes = ["B", "KB", "MB", "GB", "TB", "PB", "EB", "ZB", "YB"];
        const i = Math.floor(Math.log(bytesN) / Math.log(k));
        return `${parseFloat((bytesN / Math.pow(k, i)).toFixed(2))}${sizes[i]}ps`;
      }
      
      export function printMediaInfo(
        minfo: Record<string, any>,
        args: Record<string, any>,
      ): void {
        if (!minfo["media"]) return;
        const file = minfo["media"]["@ref"];
        const vtrack = minfo["media"]["track"].find(
          (track: any) => track["@type"] === "Video",
        );
        const atrack = minfo["media"]["track"].find(
          (track: any) => track["@type"] === "Audio",
        );
        if (!vtrack || !atrack) {
          if (args.debug) {
            console.log(`Skipping file: ${file}`);
          }
          return;
        }
      
        console.log(
          `${file}: ${prettyTime(atrack["Duration"])} | ${vtrack["Format"]} ${vtrack["Format_Profile"]}/${vtrack["Width"]}x${vtrack["Height"]}@${vtrack["FrameRate"]}/${prettyBps(vtrack["BitRate"])} | ${atrack["Format"]}/${prettyBps(atrack["BitRate"])}`,
        );
        // console.log(JSON.stringify(vinfos));
      }
      
      export const getMediainfo = async (filepath: string) => {
        const { stdout, stderr, exitCode } =
          await $`mediainfo --Output=JSON "${filepath}"`.nothrow().quiet();
        if (exitCode !== 0) {
          console.error(`Error processing file: ${filepath}`);
          console.error(stderr);
          return null;
        }
      
        const minfo = JSON.parse(stdout.toString()) as Record<string, any>;
        const vtrack = minfo["media"]["track"].find(
          (track: any) => track["@type"] === "Video",
        );
        if (vtrack) {
          if (!vtrack["BitRate"]) {
            vtrack["BitRate"] = vtrack["BitRate_Maximum"];
          }
        }
        const atrack = minfo["media"]["track"].find(
          (track: any) => track["@type"] === "Audio",
        );
        if (atrack) {
          if (!atrack["BitRate"]) {
            atrack["BitRate"] = vtrack["BitRate_Nominal"];
          }
        }
      
        return minfo;
      };
      
      export default async (filepath: string) => {
        const minfo = await getMediainfo(filepath);
        if (!minfo) return -1;
        printMediaInfo(minfo, { debug: true });
        return 0;
      };
      
  referencedBy: {}

vtrack[\"BitRate_Nominal\"];\n    }\n  }\n\n  return minfo;\n};\n\nexport function printMediaInfo(\n  minfo: Record<string, any>,\n  args: Record<string, any>,\n): void {\n  if (!minfo[\"media\"]) );\n}\n"

- path: tasks/ls/default.ts
  content: |
      import { $ } from "bun";
      
      export async function ls(filepath: string) {
        const { stdout, stderr, exitCode } = await $`ls \"${filepath}\"`
          .nothrow()
          .quiet();
        if (stdout) console.log(stdout.toString());
        if (stderr) console.error(stderr.toString());
        return exitCode === null ? 0 : exitCode;
      }
      
      /**
       * Task entrypoint for the runner's ExecutionContext contract.
       * Wraps the plain `ls(filepath)` function so the runner can capture logs
       * and drive state transitions.
       */
      export async function runWith(ctx: {
        input: { filepath: string };
        signal: AbortSignal;
        done: (exitCode: number) => void;
      }) {
        try {
          if (ctx.signal.aborted) {
            ctx.done(124);
            return;
          }
          const exitCode = await ls(ctx.input.filepath);
          ctx.done(exitCode);
        } catch (err) {
          console.error(err);
          ctx.done(1);
        }
      }
      
  referencedBy: {}
- path: tasks/minfo/default.ts
  content: |
      import { getMediainfo, printMediaInfo } from "../lib/media.ts";
      
      export async function minfo(filepath: string) {
        const minfoData = await getMediainfo(filepath);
        if (!minfoData) return -1;
        printMediaInfo(minfoData, { debug: true });
        return 0;
      }
      
      export async function runWith(ctx: {
        input: { filepath: string };
        signal: AbortSignal;
        done: (exitCode: number) => void;
      }) {
        try {
          if (ctx.signal.aborted) {
            ctx.done(124);
            return;
          }
          const exitCode = await minfo(ctx.input.filepath);
          ctx.done(exitCode);
        } catch (err) {
          console.error(err);
          ctx.done(1);
        }
      }
      
      export default runWith;
      
  referencedBy: {}

When you're ready to move from spec to implementation, the natural next step is the runner + daemon skeleton: task discovery from the search paths, the SQLite schema via Bun's built-in SQLite API, the queue state machine (IDLE/PROCESSING/STOPPED) with per-queue concurrency, the `ExecutionContext` wrapper that captures logs and drives PENDING→RUNNING→SUCCESS/FAILED, `abort` via `AbortSignal`, and a first cut of `tq daemon` + `tq add` + the REST API + SSE dashboard. The open issues in `ISSUES.md` are the natural places to make concrete choices before or during that build (especially the `runWith` vs `default` entrypoint shape, the console-capture mechanism, and the `tq` daemon-detection/degradation behavior).

## User

can you suggest appropriate tasks to be included in defualt package?

## Assistant

A good default package is a small, self-contained set of tasks that exercise the runner's features without depending on anything exotic. The goal is: one task that reads/writes files, one that talks to a process, one that is CPU/IO-bound with a reason for low concurrency, one that produces structured output you'd want to see on a dashboard, and a couple of tiny ones that prove the CLI/dashboard plumbing works.

Here's a concrete set for the `default` namespace, with the rationale for each.

---

**1. `default:ls` — list a directory** *(you already have this)*

- Input: `{ filepath: string }` (or a bare path via the CLI convenience)
- What it does: runs `ls` on the path, prints the listing
- Why it's in default: smallest possible task, proves enqueue → run → log capture. Concurrency high (e.g. 8) since it's trivial.

---

**2. `default:cat` — print a file's contents**

- Input: `{ filepath: string, limit?: number }`
- What it does: reads a file, prints it (optionally truncated to `limit` lines)
- Why: complements `ls` — now you can navigate a directory with `ls` then inspect a file with `cat`. Both are filesystem reads, so together they're a minimal "look at what's on disk" toolkit. Concurrency moderate (e.g. 4).

---

**3. `default:echo` — print a message / test output capture**

- Input: `{ message: string, repeat?: number }`
- What it does: prints `message` (optionally `repeat` times), exits 0
- Why: the canonical "is my log capture working?" task. Predictable output, no external dependencies, fast. Useful on the dashboard to confirm stdout streaming. Concurrency high (e.g. 8).

---

**4. `default:date` — emit a timestamp**

- Input: `{ format?: string }`
- What it does: prints the current time, optionally formatted
- Why: demonstrates a task that produces time-varying output even with the same input — good for seeing "live" behavior on the dashboard and for testing log streaming / staleness. Concurrency high (e.g. 8).

---

**5. `default:sleep` — wait for a duration**

- Input: `{ seconds: number }`
- What it does: sleeps, then prints "slept N seconds" and exits 0
- Why: the canonical long-running task. Lets you test `stop` (finish current), `abort` (kill + reset to PENDING), concurrency limits (queue 4 sleeps of 10s each with concurrency 2 and watch the runner drain two at a time), and RUNNING-state display on the dashboard. Concurrency low-ish (e.g. 2) so it actually demonstrates limiting.

---

**6. `default:env` — dump environment variables**

- Input: `{ filter?: string }` (optional prefix to filter by)
- What it does: prints env var names (and values, or just names if a filter is set), exits 0
- Why: useful for debugging the daemon's environment, and shows a task that inspects its runtime context rather than doing I/O. Also a good example of a task whose output you might want to redact on the dashboard (secrets). Concurrency high (e.g. 8).

---

**7. `default:sha256` — hash a file**

- Input: `{ filepath: string }`
- What it does: computes SHA-256 of a file (via `sha256sum` or Bun's built-in crypto), prints hex digest
- Why: a file-based CPU/IO task with a clean single-line structured output — the kind of thing you'd actually use this runner for. Good companion to `ls`/`cat` for a "inspect and verify files" workflow. Demonstrates a task that produces deterministic output for the same input (handy for dashboard diff/null tests). Concurrency moderate (e.g. 2–4) since hashing is IO-bound on large files.

---

**8. `default:curl` — fetch a URL**

- Input: `{ url: string, method?: string, timeout?: number, headers?: record }`
- What it does: fetches a URL (via `curl` or Bun's `fetch`), prints status + body (or a truncation), exits 0 on 2xx, non-zero otherwise
- Why: the canonical network task. Exercises a different failure mode than filesystem tasks (network errors, non-2xx responses, timeouts), gives the dashboard something to show for FAILED tasks with a meaningful error, and is a realistic "this runner is useful for batch HTTP work" example. Concurrency moderate (e.g. 4), with an explicit per-instance timeout surfaced as an input so the runner's per-instance timeout feature (from ISSUES.md) has a natural integration point.

---

**9. `default:mediainfo` — media info** *(you already have this as `minfo`)*

- Input: `{ filepath: string }`
- What it does: calls `mediainfo`, formats video/audio summary
- Why: already in the repo; a real, non-trivial task that depends on an external CLI, produces multi-line formatted output, and has a reason for concurrency 1 (CPU-bound analysis). Keep it — it's a good "real work" anchor.

---

**10. `default:run` — run an arbitrary command**

- Input: `{ command: string, args?: string[], timeout?: number, cwd?: string }`
- What it does: spawns the command with the given args, captures stdout/stderr, exits with the command's exit code
- Why: the meta-task — lets a user enqueue arbitrary CLI work without writing a task folder. This is the task that makes the runner useful as a generic CLI queue, and it's the natural home for the "user add task with context" use case from the prompt when the user doesn't want to author a task definition. It also blurs the line between "task" and "one-off command", which is worth thinking about (see issues below). Concurrency configurable, default moderate (e.g. 2–4); per-instance timeout here is especially important.

---

**11. `default:health` — dashboard liveness probe**

- Input: none (or `{ ok: true }` for symmetry)
- What it does: prints "ok" + a timestamp, exits 0 instantly
- Why: not user-facing work — it's a task you enqueue from the dashboard or a monitor to confirm the runner is alive and processing. Tiny, fast, high concurrency. Also a good smoke test for the whole pipeline (enqueue → run → log → dashboard) independent of the other tasks' semantics.

---

That gives you 11 default tasks, of which 2 already exist (`ls`, `minfo`) and 9 are new. Together they cover: filesystem read (`ls`, `cat`, `sha256`), stdout/text output (`echo`, `date`, `env`, `cat`), waiting/long-running (`sleep`), network (`curl`), arbitrary command execution (`run`), environment inspection (`env`), deterministic hashing (`sha256`), formatted real-world output (`mediainfo`), and a liveness probe (`health`).

**Convenience grouping for the dashboard:** you'd want the dashboard to let you filter by "kind" — e.g. `fs` (ls, cat, sha256), `text` (echo, date, env), `network` (curl), `meta` (run, health), `media` (mediainfo), `wait` (sleep). That's just a `category` field in `settings.json` / the CLI descriptor, not a new concept.

**A few design tensions these tasks surface (log them as issues if you want):**

- **`default:run` vs. "a task is a folder"**: `run` is a task that runs arbitrary commands — it's the one task that could make authoring a task folder feel optional for simple CLI work. Worth deciding whether `run` is in scope for the default package or whether it belongs as a built-in that's always available regardless of loaded packages.
- **`curl` vs. `run` overlap**: a user could do `tq add default:run 'curl ...'` instead of `tq add default:curl ...`. The case for a dedicated `curl` task is nicer input schema (URL validated, timeout declared, structured output) and better dashboard metadata; the case against is duplication. If `run` exists, `curl` is partly redundant — decide whether `curl` stays for ergonomics/schema or gets dropped.
- **Per-instance timeout**: `sleep`, `curl`, and `run` all want an explicit timeout. If the runner gets a per-instance timeout (from ISSUES.md), these are the tasks that justify it. If not, they'll want an input-level timeout they enforce themselves.
- **Secrets in `env` output**: `env` dumps environment, which may include secrets. Worth deciding whether the dashboard redacts env output, or whether `env` only prints names by default.
- **`sha256` implementation**: Bun has built-in crypto (`Bun.crypto` / Web Crypto), so `sha256` doesn't need to shell out to `sha256sum` — but if you want tasks to demonstrate spawning child commands, having some tasks use `$` and some use pure Bun is a useful mix. Decide whether `sha256` is a pure-Bun task or a `$`-based one for consistency with `ls`/`minfo`/`curl`.

If you want, I can turn this into concrete `settings.json` + `schema.json` + `default.ts` stubs for whichever of these you want in the default package, and add them alongside the existing `ls`/`minfo`.

## User

add sha256, cat, env
hide sensitive info from cat and env

{
  "path": "tasks/sha256/default.ts",
  "instructions": "Entrypoint for the sha256 task.",
  "content": "import { createHash } from \"node:crypto\";\nimport { createReadStream } from \"node:fs\";\n\nexport async function sha256(filepath: string): Promise<string> {\n  return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {\n    const hash = createHash(\"sha256\");\n    const stream = createReadStream(filepath);\n\n    stream.on(\"data\", (chunk: Buffer) => hash.update(chunk));\n    stream.on(\"end\", () => resolve(hash.digest(\"hex\")));\n    stream.on(\"error\", reject);\n  });\n}\n\nexport async function runWith(ctx: {\n  input: { filepath: string };\n  signal: AbortSignal;\n  done: (exitCode: number) => void;\n}) {\n  try {\n    if (ctx.signal.aborted) {\n      ctx.done(124);\n      return;\n    }\n    const digest = await sha256(ctx.input.filepath);\n    console.log(digest);\n    ctx.done(0);\n  } catch (err: any) {\n    if (err?.code === \"ABORT_ERR\" || ctx.signal.aborted) {\n      ctx.done(124);\n      return;\n    }\n    console.error(`sha256: ${err?.message ?? err}`);\n    ctx.done(1);\n  }\n}\n\nexport default runWith;\n"
}
```

This is intended to make log inspection on the dashboard safer, not to guarantee no secret ever leaks.\n\n## Usage\n\n```sh\ntq add default:cat <filepath>\ntq add default:cat '{\"filepath\":\"fixture/1/1.srt\"}'\ntq add default:cat '{\"filepath\":\"fixture/1/1.srt\",\"limit\":20}'\n```\n\n## Input\n\n| Field    | Type    | Required | Description                              |\n|----------|---------|----------|------------------------------------------|\n| filepath | string  | yes      | Path to the file to print                |\n| limit    | integer | no       | Maximum number of lines to print (0 = no limit) |\n\n## Settings\n\n- Concurrency: 4\n- Category: fs\n"

{
  "path": "tasks/cat/default.ts",
  "instructions": "Entrypoint for the cat task with sensitive-info redaction.",
  "content": "import { createReadStream } from \"node:fs\";\nimport { createInterface } from \"node:readline\";\n\nconst SECRET_PATTERNS = [\n  // AWS access key IDs\n  /AKIA[0-9A-Z]{16,}/i,\n  // AWS secret access keys (rough)\n  /(?<=[A-Z0-9/+=]{40})/,\n  // Generic long base64-ish tokens\n  /\\b([A-Za-z0-9+/]{40,}={0,2})\\b/,\n  // API keys / tokens labeled nearby\n  /(api[_-]?key|apikey|access[_-]?key|secret[_-]?key|token|password|passwd|pwd|auth|credential|private[_-]?key)\\s*[=:]\\s*['\"]?([^\\s'\"]{8,})['\"]?/gi,\n  // Bearer / Basic auth headers\n  /(Bearer|Basic)\\s+([A-Za-z0-9+/=]{20,})/gi,\n  // Private key blocks\n  /-----BEGIN (RSA |EC |OPENSSH |DSA |ENCRYPTED )?PRIVATE KEY-----[\\s\\S]*?-----END (RSA |EC |OPENSSH |DSA |ENCRYPTED )?PRIVATE KEY-----/gi,\n  // JWTs\n  /\\beyJ[A-Za-z0-9_-]{10,}\\.[A-Za-z0-9_-]{10,}\\.[A-Za-z0-9_-]{10,}\\b/gi,\n  // GitHub-style tokens\n  /gh[pousr]_[A-Za-z0-9]{36,}/gi,\n  // Generic password= / secret= assignments\n  /\\b(?:password|passwd|pwd|secret|api_key|apikey|access_key|token)\\s*=\\s*['\"]?([^'\";\\s]{6,})['\"]?/gi,\n];\n\nfunction redactLine(line: string): string {\n  let result = line;\n  for (const pattern of SECRET_PATTERNS) {\n    result = result.replace(pattern, (match) => {\n      // For labeled patterns, keep the label, redact the value\n      const labeled = /^(api[_-]?key|apikey|access[_-]?key|secret[_-]?key|token|password|passwd|pwd|auth|credential|private[_-]?key|bearer|basic)\\s*[=:]\\s*['\"]?/i;\n      const m = match.match(labeled);\n      if (m && m[0]) {\n        return m[0] + \"[REDACTED]\";\n      }\n      return \"[REDACTED]\";\n    });\n  }\n  return result;\n}\n\nexport async function cat(\n  filepath: string,\n  limit: number = 0,\n): Promise<{ lines: string[]; redacted: number }> {\n  const lines: string[] = [];\n  let redacted = 0;\n  const rl = createInterface({\n    input: createReadStream(filepath),\n    crlfDelay: Number.POSITIVE_INFINITY,\n  });\n\n  for await (const line of rl) {\n    if (limit > 0 && lines.length >= limit) break;\n    const redactedLine = redactLine(line);\n    if (redactedLine !== line) redacted++;\n    lines.push(redactedLine);\n  }\n\n  return { lines, redacted };\n}\n\nexport async function runWith(ctx: {\n  input: { filepath: string; limit?: number };\n  signal: AbortSignal;\n  done: (exitCode: number) => void;\n}) {\n  try {\n    if (ctx.signal.aborted) {\n      ctx.done(124);\n      return;\n    }\n    const limit = ctx.input.limit ?? 0;\n    const { lines, redacted } = await cat(ctx.input.filepath, limit);\n    for (const line of lines) console.log(line);\n    if (redacted > 0) {\n      console.error(`cat: redacted ${redacted} sensitive value(s)`);\n    }\n    ctx.done(0);\n  } catch (err: any) {\n    if (err?.code === \"ENOENT\") {\n      console.error(`cat: file not found: ${ctx.input.filepath}`);\n      ctx.done(1);\n      return;\n    }\n    if (ctx.signal.aborted) {\n      ctx.done(124);\n      return;\n    }\n    console.error(`cat: ${err?.message ?? err}`);\n    ctx.done(1);\n  }\n}\n\nexport default runWith;\n"
}
```

```json
{
  "path": "tasks/env/default.ts",
  "instructions": "Entrypoint for the env task with sensitive-info redaction.",
  "content": "const SENSITIVE_NAMES = new Set([\n  \"aws_secret_access_key\",\n  \"aws_access_key_id\",\n  \"aws_session_token\",\n  \"password\",\n  \"passwd\",\n  \"pwd\",\n  \"secret\",\n  \"secret_key\",\n  \"secret_access_key\",\n  \"api_key\",\n  \"apikey\",\n  \"api_key_id\",\n  \"access_key\",\n  \"access_token\",\n  \"refresh_token\",\n  \"token\",\n  \"auth_token\",\n  \"private_key\",\n  \"private_key_pem\",\n  \"ssh_key\",\n  \"ssh_private_key\",\n  \"jwt_secret\",\n  \"jwt_signing_key\",\n  \"client_secret\",\n  \"client_password\",\n  \"credentials\",\n  \"mongodb_uri\",\n  \"mongodb_connection_string\",\n  \"database_url\",\n  \"db_password\",\n  \"redis_password\",\n  \"redispassword\",\n  \"smtp_password\",\n  \"mail_password\",\n  \"stripe_secret_key\",\n  \"stripe_key\",\n  \"slack_bot_token\",\n  \"slack_token\",\n  \"github_token\",\n  \"gh_token\",\n  \"pat\",\n  \"personal_access_token\",\n]);\n\nconst SENSITIVE_VALUE_PREFIXES = [\n  \"sk-\", // OpenAI-style secret keys\n  \"ghp_\", \"gho_\", \"ghu_\", \"ghs_\", \"ghr_\", // GitHub tokens\n  \"xoxb-\", \"xoxp-\", \"xoxa-\", \"xoxr-\", // Slack\n  \"sk_live_\", \"sk_test_\", // Stripe\n  \"AKIA\", // AWS access key id\n  \"-----BEGIN\", // private key headers\n  \"ya29.\", // Google service account\n  \"Bot \", // Discord bot tokens\n  \"N2\", // some JWT-ish\n];\n\nfunction isSensitiveName(name: string): boolean {\n  const lower = name.toLowerCase();\n  if (SENSITIVE_NAMES.has(lower)) return true;\n  if (lower.includes(\"password\")) return true;\n  if (lower.includes(\"secret\")) return true;\n  if (lower.includes(\"token\") && !lower.includes(\"accesstoken\")) return true;\n  if (lower.includes(\"api_key\") || lower.includes(\"apikey\")) return true;\n  if (lower.includes(\"private_key\")) return true;\n  return false;\n}\n\nfunction isSensitiveValue(value: string): boolean {\n  if (!value || value.length < 8) return false;\n  const upper = value.toUpperCase();\n  if (upper.startsWith(\"AKIA\")) return true;\n  for (const prefix of SENSITIVE_VALUE_PREFIXES) {\n    if (value.startsWith(prefix)) return true;\n  }\n  // long base64-ish blob\n  if (/^[A-Za-z0-9+/]{40,}={0,2}$/.test(value)) return true;\n  // looks like a private key block\n  if (value.includes(\"-----BEGIN\") && value.includes(\"PRIVATE KEY-----\")) return true;\n  // JWT\n  if (/^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{10,}\\.[A-Za-z0-9_-]{10,}\\.[A-Za-z0-9_-]{10,}$/.test(value)) return true;\n  return false;\n}\n\nfunction redact(value: string): string {\n  return \"[REDACTED]\";\n}\n\nexport async function env(opts: { filter?: string; values?: boolean }): Promise<void> {\n  const { filter, values = true } = opts;\n  const entries = Object.entries(process.env).sort(([a], [b]) =>\n    a.localeCompare(b),\n  );\n\n  for (const [name, value] of entries) {\n    if (filter && !name.startsWith(filter)) continue;\n    const sensitive = isSensitiveName(name) || (value !== undefined && isSensitiveValue(value));\n    if (values) {\n      if (sensitive) {\n        console.log(`${name}=${redact(value ?? \"\")}`);\n      } else {\n        console.log(`${name}=${value ?? \"\"}`);\n      }\n    } else {\n      console.log(name);\n    }\n  }\n}\n\nexport async function runWith(ctx: {\n  input: { filter?: string; values?: boolean };\n  signal: AbortSignal;\n  done: (exitCode: number) => void;\n}) {\n  try {\n    if (ctx.signal.aborted) {\n      ctx.done(124);\n      return;\n    }\n    await env({\n      filter: ctx.input.filter,\n      values: ctx.input.values ?? true,\n    });\n    ctx.done(0);\n  } catch (err: any) {\n    if (ctx.signal.aborted) {\n      ctx.done(124);\n      return;\n    }\n    console.error(`env: ${err?.message ?? err}`);\n    ctx.done(1);\n  }\n}\n\nexport default runWith;\n"
}
```

````

- path: SPEC.md
  content: |
      # Task Runner — Specification
      
      ## 1. Overview
      
      A Bun-based CLI daemon that registers **tasks** (from folders on a search path, like `$PATH`), enqueues **task instances** via `tq add <namespace:id> <context>`, and runs them with per-queue concurrency limits. A web dashboard (served by the daemon) shows queue/task state and streams logs in real time. State and metadata persist to SQLite (via Bun's built-in SQLite API); logs are files on disk.
      
      This document captures the design settled in discussion and the open issues still to clarify.
      
      ---
      
      ## 2. Glossary
      
      - **Task definition** — a registered task: its identity (`namespace:id`), input schema, settings, entrypoint, README, assets.
      - **Task instance** — one execution of a task definition: a PENDING → RUNNING → SUCCESS/FAILED lifecycle with captured stdout/stderr, timing, and exit code.
      - **Task queue** — one per task definition. Holds PENDING instances and tracks RUNNING count against the concurrency limit. Queue states: IDLE / PROCESSING / STOPPED.
      - **Task package** — a Bun package that can contain multiple task definitions under a `tasks/` directory, plus shared code at the package root.
      - **Search path** — a directory the daemon walks to discover task definitions (project `tasks/`, user `~/.taskrunner/tasks/`, installed-package locations).
      - **Namespace** — a task identity prefix separated by `:`. `default` is the implicit namespace for project + user search paths; other namespaces come from installed packages.
      - **Context / input** — the structured payload a task instance receives. Validated against the task's input schema at enqueue time.
      - **Runtime descriptor** — per-"runtime" metadata for a task (daemon view, CLI view), modeled on agent-skills' `agents/<runtime>.yaml`.
      
      ---
      
      ## 3. Architecture — settled decisions
      
      ### 3.1 Process model
      
      - Single daemon process (`tq daemon`).
      - Task instances run as concurrent coroutines **in the same process**, launched by the runner with an `AbortSignal` and wrapped in error handling.
      - Tasks are **trusted code** (installed by the user). Process isolation is future hardening, not today's scope.
      - Bun's strengths are leveraged directly: Bun's built-in SQLite API, `Bun.serve`, `Bun.$` / `$` for spawning child commands inside a task, `Bun.sleep` / event-loop scheduling.
      
      ### 3.2 Task identity and lookup
      
      - Identity is `namespace:id`, e.g. `default:minfo`, `acme:encode`. The `:` separator is used from the start.
      - A task is **one folder** containing at minimum: `default.ts` (entrypoint), `README.md`, `settings.json` (task settings), and optionally `schema.json` (input schema) and `agents/<runtime>.json` (runtime descriptors).
      - A **task package** is a Bun package whose `tasks/` directory holds one subfolder per task. Shared code lives at the package root (or `lib/`) and tasks import it via relative paths.
      - Discovery is `$PATH`-like: the daemon walks search paths, reads each task folder's manifest, and builds an index mapping `namespace:id` → folder location + manifest.
      - Identity (namespace + id) is **declared in the task's manifest**, not inferred from the folder name.
      
      ### 3.3 Input / context convention
      
      - Each task declares its input shape via `schema.json` (JSON Schema draft-07) referenced from `settings.json` (or inlined).
      - At `tq add <namespace:id> <context>`, the CLI sends the context; the registry parses + validates it against the schema before enqueueing.
      - The task's `default` receives the **parsed, validated input object**.
      - CLI convenience: when the schema has exactly one required string property, `tq add <id> <bare-string>` auto-wraps to `{"<prop>": "<bare-string>"}`. The authoritative input is always JSON validated against the schema.
      - Existing sample tasks (`ls`, `minfo`) declare `{ type: "object", properties: { filepath: { type: "string" } }, required: ["filepath"] }` and are used as `tq add default:ls '{"filepath":"fixture/1"}'` (or the convenience form `tq add default:ls fixture/1`).
      
      ### 3.4 Execution contract
      
      - Runner invokes: `default(ctx: ExecutionContext) → Promise<void>`.
      - `ExecutionContext` provides: parsed input, `AbortSignal` (for `abort`), a stdout/stderr capture mechanism (streams or log callbacks), and a completion signal (callback or thrown error).
      - Runner owns **observability**: captures stdout/stderr, measures duration, transitions state. Task owns **execution**.
      - One `default` invocation = one task instance = one lifecycle. "Task implements its own scheduling" means the task logic may internally schedule sub-work, but from the runner's view it is one atomic instance.
      - Exit mapping: runner treats clean completion with exit code 0 as SUCCESS; non-zero exit or thrown error as FAILED. `abort` cancels the coroutine (and any child process the task spawned via `$`), preserves partial logs, and resets the instance to PENDING for retry.
      
      ### 3.5 Queue model
      
      - One queue per task definition. Queue ID == task identity.
      - Per-queue concurrency configured in `settings.json` (e.g. `"concurrency": 3`).
      - Queue states: IDLE ⇄ PROCESSING ⇄ STOPPED.
        - IDLE: queue exists, no RUNNING tasks, not actively draining (empty or freshly created).
        - PROCESSING: actively draining PENDING tasks up to concurrency.
        - STOPPED: finished current RUNNING tasks, will not start next PENDING.
      - `stop`: complete current RUNNING, do not start next PENDING → PROCESSING → STOPPED.
      - `start`: resume → STOPPED → PROCESSING (PENDING tasks remain and are picked up in order).
      - `abort`: kill current RUNNING, reset to PENDING, do not start next PENDING.
      - Drain order: FIFO / insertion order.
      
      ### 3.6 Persistence
      
      - SQLite via Bun's built-in SQLite API. DB holds: task definition registry, task instances (state, input, exitCode, timing, error), queue state, concurrency, running count.
      - Logs (stdout/stderr) are **files on disk** in `logs/` (already in `.gitignore`), referenced by path from the DB. Reasoning: logs can be large; SQLite is for structured lookups/dashboard queries.
      - DB location: configurable, default `./taskrunner.db` in the daemon's working directory (per-project DB); override via `--db` flag or `TASKRUNNER_DB` env var.
      
      ### 3.7 Autostart
      
      - "Autostart" = on `tq daemon`, all registered queues automatically enter PROCESSING and begin draining PENDING tasks.
      - No systemd/reboot autostart in scope.
      - `tq add` writes to the DB directly, so tasks can be enqueued before the daemon runs; they are picked up on `tq daemon` or `tq start`.
      
      ### 3.8 Dashboard
      
      - Web UI served by the daemon's HTTP server (Bun.serve), minimal HTML at `/`.
      - Real-time updates via SSE (state changes + log tailing).
      - REST API on the same server:
        - `GET /` — dashboard HTML
        - `GET /api/queues` — list queues
        - `GET /api/queues/:id` — queue detail (state, concurrency, running count, task count)
        - `GET /api/queues/:id/tasks` — tasks in queue (with state)
        - `GET /api/tasks/:id` — task instance detail
        - `GET /api/tasks/:id/logs` — log file content
        - `GET /api/tasks/:id/logs/stream` — SSE log stream
        - `POST /api/queues/:id/start` — start queue
        - `POST /api/queues/:id/stop` — stop queue
        - `POST /api/queues/:id/abort` — abort current instance
        - `POST /api/tasks` — enqueue task (API equivalent of `tq add`)
      
      ### 3.9 CLI (`tq`)
      
      - Same binary, two modes:
        - `tq daemon` — start HTTP server + runner loop.
        - `tq <subcommand>` — add, stop, start, abort, clear, status, logs, install, reload.
      - `tq` talks to a running daemon over HTTP when available; degrades to direct SQLite/DB access when the daemon is not running.
      - `tq add` writes to the DB directly (works without the daemon).
      - Sample workflow (from the prompt, adapted to namespace:id):
      
      ```sh
      tq daemon          # starts service; registered tasks autostart
      
      tq add default:ls fixture/1
      tq add default:ls fixture/2
      tq add default:minfo fixture/1
      tq add default:minfo fixture/2
      
      tq stop default:ls
      tq start default:ls
      tq abort default:minfo
      
      tq clear default:ls        # clear SUCCESS tasks
      tq clear --all default:ls  # clear all tasks
      ```
      
      ---
      
      ## 4. File and directory layout
      
      ### 4.1 Project layout (this repo)
      
      ```
      .
      ├── index.ts            # daemon entrypoint (TBD)
      ├── package.json
      ├── bun.lock
      ├── tsconfig.json
      ├── SPEC.md             # this file
      ├── tasks/              # project task search path (default namespace)
      │   ├── ls/
      │   │   ├── default.ts
      │   │   ├── README.md
      │   │   ├── settings.json
      │   │   └── schema.json
      │   └── minfo/
      │       ├── default.ts
      │       ├── README.md
      │       ├── settings.json
      │       └── schema.json
      ├── fixture/
      │   ├── 1/1.srt
      │   └── 2/2.srt
      ├── logs/               # task instance logs (gitignored)
      └── taskrunner.db       # SQLite DB (gitignored, configurable via --db / TASKRUNNER_DB)
      ```
      
      ### 4.2 Task folder contents
      
      A task folder (e.g. `tasks/minfo/`) contains:
      
      | File | Purpose |
      |---|---|
      | `index.ts` | Entrypoint: exports `runWith(ctx: ExecutionContext) => Promise<void>` and `default = runWith` |
      | `README.md` | Human documentation for the task |
      | `settings.json` | Task settings: concurrency, env, inputSchema ref, schedule ref, metadata, category |
      | `schema.json` | *(optional)* JSON Schema for the input; if absent, input is an opaque string |
      | `agents/<runtime>.json` | *(optional)* per-runtime descriptor (daemon, cli, …) |
      
      A task **package** root (e.g. an installable bun package) additionally has:
      
      ```
      my-package/
      ├── package.json          # bun package: name, version, dependencies, "tasks": ["ls","minfo"]
      ├── lib/                  # shared code imported by tasks via relative paths
      │   └── media.ts
      └── tasks/
          ├── ls/
          └── minfo/
      ```
      
      ### 4.3 Lockfile
      
      A lockfile (e.g. `.taskrunner.lock` or `taskrunner-lock.json`) maps `namespace:id` → `{ source, installedPath, manifestHash }`, analogous to agent-skills' `skills-lock.json`. The daemon reads it to know installed-package task locations.
      
      ---
      
      ## 5. Task manifest and schema — illustrative shape
      
      ### 5.1 `settings.json` (task settings)
      
      ```json
      {
        "namespace": "default",
        "id": "minfo",
        "name": "Media info",
        "description": "Print media information for a video/audio file",
        "concurrency": 1,
        "inputSchema": "schema.json",
        "env": {},
        "agents": {
          "daemon": { "logPath": "logs" },
          "cli": { "argumentHint": "<filepath>", "example": "tq add default:minfo fixture/1" }
        }
      }
      ```
      
      ### 5.2 `schema.json` (input schema)
      
      ```json
      {
        "$schema": "http://json-schema.org/draft-07/schema#",
        "type": "object",
        "properties": {
          "filepath": { "type": "string" }
        },
        "required": ["filepath"]
      }
      ```
      
      ### 5.3 `agents/cli.json` (CLI runtime descriptor)
      
      ```json
      {
        "display_name": "Media info",
        "short_description": "Print media information for a video/audio file",
        "argument_hint": "<filepath>",
        "example": "tq add default:minfo fixture/1"
      }
      ```
      
      ---
      
      ## 6. ExecutionContext interface (TypeScript shape)
      
      ```ts
      interface ExecutionContext<TInput> {
        input: TInput;
        signal: AbortSignal;
        stdout: WritableStream | LogSink;
        stderr: WritableStream | LogSink;
        done: (exitCode: number) => void;
      }
      ```
      
      The runner wraps `default(ctx)` and, regardless of outcome, transitions the instance state and captures logs/timing. The task calls `ctx.done(0)` on success or throws / calls `ctx.done(nonZero)` on failure.
      
      ---
      
      ## 7. State machine summary
      
      ### 7.1 Task instance states
      
      PENDING → RUNNING → SUCCESS
      PENDING → RUNNING → FAILED
      RUNNING → (abort) → PENDING (retry, partial logs preserved)
      
      ### 7.2 Queue states
      
      IDLE → PROCESSING (when a task is launched and queue becomes active)
      PROCESSING → STOPPED (on `stop`)
      STOPPED → PROCESSING (on `start`)
      PROCESSING → IDLE (when queue empties and no RUNNING tasks remain)
      
      ---
      
      ## 8. CLI command reference (settled)
      
      | Command | Meaning |
      |---|---|
      | `tq daemon` | Start the daemon (HTTP server + runner loop); queues autostart |
      | `tq add <namespace:id> <context>` | Enqueue a task instance (writes DB directly) |
      | `tq stop <namespace:id>` | Stop queue: finish RUNNING, don't start next PENDING |
      | `tq start <namespace:id>` | Resume queue: start processing PENDING |
      | `tq abort <namespace:id>` | Kill current RUNNING, reset to PENDING, don't start next |
      | `tq clear <namespace:id>` | Delete SUCCESS instances (+ logs) for the queue |
      | `tq clear --all <namespace:id>` | Delete all instances (except RUNNING) + logs |
      | `tq status` | Show queue/instance state (from DB or daemon API) |
      | `tq logs <instance-id>` | Show task instance logs |
      | `tq install <package>` | Install a task package, extract tasks/, update lockfile |
      | `tq reload` | Reload task discovery from search paths (SIGHUP alternative) |
      
      ---
      
      ## 9. Open issues / further discussion
      
      These are still to clarify or implement; listed here as requested ("log issues we would further discuss and clarify").
      
      ### 9.1 Input schema — inference vs. explicit
      
      - **Issue**: We chose explicit `schema.json` over inferring the input shape from the TS signature of `default`. Confirm this is right, and decide whether to support a *minimal* task (no schema → input is a plain string) as a first-class convenience, or always require a schema.
      - **Impact**: affects `tq add` parsing logic and how bare-string convenience wrapping works.
      
      ### 9.2 `ExecutionContext` capture mechanism
      
      - **Issue**: The `stdout`/`stderr` capture in `ExecutionContext` is underspecified: is it a `WritableStream` the task writes to, a `log(str, isErr)` callback the task calls, or does the runner intercept `console.log`/`console.error` globally for the task's execution? Each has different ergonomics for tasks that use `console.log` (like the current `minfo.ts`).
      - **Impact**: affects the task entrypoint contract and how existing sample tasks are adapted.
      
      ### 9.3 Same-process isolation guarantees
      
      - **Issue**: We chose same-process task execution with trusted tasks. Need to define what happens on: unhandled error (runner catches → FAILED), infinite loop (instance hangs → abort required), memory leak (no isolation → affects daemon). Decide whether to document these as "trusted tasks" caveats now, or add a lightweight per-instance timeout as a safety net.
      - **Impact**: affects robustness posture and whether `tq abort` needs a per-instance timeout.
      
      ### 9.4 `tq` degradation behavior
      
      - **Issue**: `tq` talks HTTP to the daemon when available, direct DB otherwise. Open: how does `tq` detect the daemon is running? (TODO: check a PID file, a lock file, or try the API and fall back.) And does `tq add` via direct DB require the daemon to be notified (e.g. a wake-up signal) or is polling on the daemon side fine?
      - **Impact**: affects CLI implementation and daemon wake-up semantics.
      
      ### 9.5 Log retention and clear semantics
      
      - **Issue**: `clear` is destructive (delete instance rows + log files). Open: should there be a retention policy (e.g. auto-clear SUCCESS older than N days), a non-destructive archive (`tq archive`), or per-instance log rotation? Also: can `clear --all` remove RUNNING instances? (Currently: no — refuses/aborts RUNNING.)
      - **Impact**: affects `tq clear` behavior and log lifecycle.
      
      ### 9.6 Dashboard — command exposure
      
      - **Issue**: The dashboard is read-only + SSE; commands go through CLI or REST API. Open: do we want in-dashboard controls (start/stop/abort buttons) that call the REST API from the browser, or keep the dashboard strictly observational and push all ops to the CLI?
      - **Impact**: affects dashboard UI scope.
      
      ### 9.7 Task package install mechanics
      
      - **Issue**: `tq install <package>` is sketched as a convenience (bun install + extract `tasks/` + update lockfile). Open: where do installed package tasks land — `node_modules/<pkg>/tasks/` (discovered via the lockfile) or an extracted copy in `~/.taskrunner/tasks/<pkg>/`? And how are package dependencies of a task (e.g. a task that `import`s a third-party lib) resolved — does the daemon need the package installed, or are tasks bundled?
      - **Impact**: affects install path, lockfile semantics, and module resolution for tasks.
      
      ### 9.8 Namespace shadowing and search-path order
      
      - **Issue**: Search paths are walked in order (project `tasks/`, user `~/.taskrunner/tasks/`, installed-package locations). Open: is the first match the winner ( shadowing ), or do we merge/warn on conflicts? And can a user task in `~/.taskrunner/tasks/` shadow a project task with the same `namespace:id`?
      - **Impact**: affects discovery semantics and conflict behavior.
      
      ### 9.9 Daemon configuration
      
      - **Issue**: Configuration is underspecified: DB path, HTTP port, search paths, log directory, concurrency defaults. Open: is there a config file (`taskrunner.toml` / `taskrunner.json` / `.taskrunnerrc`), env vars, CLI flags, or a mix? And where does it live (project-level, user-level)?
      - **Impact**: affects startup and portability.
      
      ### 9.10 `tq reload` and live task discovery
      
      - **Issue**: We have `tq reload` (re-walk search paths, e.g. on SIGHUP). Open: does reload also re-read task manifests/schemas (so a changed `settings.json` takes effect without restart), or is reload only for newly added/removed task folders?
      - **Impact**: affects operational model for updating tasks.
      
      ### 9.11 Sample tasks adaptation
      
      - **Issue (resolved in sample)**: The sample tasks were `tasks/ls.ts` and `tasks/minfo.ts` exporting `default (filepath: string) => Promise<number>`. They have been restructured into task folders (`tasks/ls/`, `tasks/minfo/`) with `default.ts` entrypoints exposing `runWith(ctx)` + `default`, plus `README.md`, `settings.json`, `schema.json`. The minfo task now imports shared helpers from `tasks/lib/media.ts`, demonstrating the package-level sharing model.
      
      - **Still open**: whether to keep the `runWith(ctx)` + `default` dual export or consolidate to a single `default(ctx)` export as the canonical entrypoint.
      - **Impact**: affects the first batch of implemented tasks and whether the samples still work as before.
      
      ---
      
      ## 10. Out of scope (for now)
      
      - Systemd/reboot autostart of the daemon.
      - Per-instance process isolation (child-process sandboxing).
      - Priority queues, explicit priorities, weighted scheduling.
      - Non-destructive archive / retention policies (defer to `tq archive` + retention later).
      - WebSocket bidirectional dashboard (SSE is enough for now).
      - Multiple concurrent runtimes/agents per task beyond the daemon + CLI descriptors.
      - Service tasks / long-running persistent workers (one instance = one `default` invocation for now).
      
  referencedBy: {}
````

# Open Issues — Task Runner

Issues to discuss/clarify further,logged as requested. Each has an owner-ish note on impact and a suggested next step.

## Input schema — inference vs. explicit

- **What's unsettled**: We chose explicit `schema.json` over inferring the input shape from the TS signature of `default`. Not yet decided whether a minimal task (no schema → input is a plain string) is a first-class convenience, or whether a schema is always required.
- **Impact**: `tq add` parsing logic; how the bare-string convenience wrapping works; whether `ls`/`minfo`/`sha256` (single-string input) need `schema.json` or can get by with a string schema.
- **Suggested next step**: Decide on a "string schema" shortcut (e.g. `settings.json` with `"inputType": "string"`) so simple tasks (ls, sha256, cat) don't need a full `schema.json`, then confirm `tq add` wrapping rules.

## ExecutionContext capture mechanism

- **What's unsettled**: How stdout/stderr are captured for the task. Options: (a) `WritableStream` the task writes to, (b) a `log(str, isErr)` callback the task calls, (c) the runner intercepts `console.log`/`console.error` globally for the task's execution scope.
- **Impact**: task entrypoint ergonomics; existing tasks that use `console.log` (e.g. `minfo.ts` does a lot of `console.log`) need to keep working without rewriting every log statement.
- **Suggested next step**: Pick (c) for ergonomics (runner temporarily redirects `console.log`/`console.error` per instance), or (b) if we want explicit log control. Avoid (a) unless tasks opt in — most tasks currently just print. Note: the `cat` and `env` tasks already do their own redaction on output, which is the task-side analogue of the dashboard-side concern — worth reconciling which layer owns redaction (task, runner, or dashboard).

## Same-process isolation guarantees

- **What's unsettled**: Same-process task execution with trusted tasks. Unclear what happens on unhandled error (runner catches → FAILED — probably fine), infinite loop (instance hangs → abort required), and memory leak (no isolation → affects daemon).
- **Impact**: robustness posture; whether `tq abort` needs a per-instance timeout; whether we document "trusted tasks" caveats now.
- **Suggested next step**: Add a per-instance timeout (configurable, default e.g. 30 min) as a safety net, and document the trusted-tasks caveat. Revisit process isolation later if needed.

## `tq` degradation behavior

- **What's unsettled**: How `tq` detects the daemon is running (PID file? lock file? try the API and fall back?), and whether `tq add` via direct DB needs to wake the daemon or polling is fine.
- **Impact**: CLI implementation; daemon wake-up semantics; whether `tq add` can "miss" a running daemon that doesn't poll fast enough.
- **Suggested next step**: Use a lock/PID file + API probe; daemon polls DB for PENDING on a short interval (e.g. 1s) so `tq add` without daemon is fine and `tq add` with daemon picks up quickly.

## Log retention and clear semantics

- **What's unsettled**: `clear` is destructive. Unclear whether to add auto-clear of old SUCCESS tasks, a non-destructive `tq archive`, or per-instance log rotation. Also whether `clear --all` can remove RUNNING (currently: no).
- **Impact**: `tq clear` behavior; log lifecycle; disk usage over time.
- **Suggested next step**: Keep `clear` destructive for now, refuse RUNNING on `--all`, and defer archive/retention to a later `tq archive` command.

## Dashboard — command exposure

- **What's unsettled**: Whether the dashboard gets in-page start/stop/abort buttons (calling the REST API from the browser) or stays observational with all ops on the CLI.
- **Impact**: dashboard UI scope; whether the REST API endpoints for start/stop/abort need auth/rate-limiting (they don't if only CLI uses them, but do if the dashboard calls them).
- **Suggested next step**: Start observational; add in-dashboard controls later if wanted. REST API for start/stop/abort is still useful as the CLI's backend and for future tooling.

## Task package install mechanics

- **What's unsettled**: Where installed package tasks land — `node_modules/<pkg>/tasks/` discovered via lockfile, or an extracted copy in `~/.taskrunner/tasks/<pkg>/`. Also how a task's own npm dependencies are resolved (does the daemon need the package installed, or are tasks bundled).
- **Impact**: install path; lockfile semantics; module resolution for tasks; whether `tq install` is just a bun add + symlink or a real extraction.
- **Suggested next step**: Start with `node_modules/<pkg>/tasks/` discovered via lockfile (no extraction; bun resolution handles deps). Add extraction to a user dir later if isolation/isolation-from-project is wanted.

## Namespace shadowing and search-path order

- **What's unsettled**: Search paths walked in order (project `tasks/`, user `~/.taskrunner/tasks/`, installed-package locations). Unclear if first match wins (shadowing) or if we merge/warn on conflicts, and whether a user task can shadow a project task with the same `namespace:id`.
- **Impact**: discovery semantics; conflict behavior; predictability for users.
- **Suggested next step**: First match wins (project can't shadow user by default? or project wins over user?). Decide order and shadowing rules, then warn on conflicts at `tq reload`.

## Daemon configuration

- **What's unsettled**: How config is expressed (config file `taskrunner.toml`/`taskrunner.json`/`.taskrunnerrc`, env vars, CLI flags, or a mix), and where it lives (project-level, user-level).
- **Impact**: startup behavior; portability; per-project vs. global daemon behavior.
- **Suggested next step**: Project-level config file (`taskrunner.toml`) for DB path, HTTP port, search paths, log dir, concurrency defaults; CLI flags override; env vars as a fallback. No user-level config for now.

## `tq reload` and live task discovery

- **What's unsettled**: Whether `tq reload` re-reads task manifests/schemas (so a changed `settings.json` takes effect without restart) or only picks up newly added/removed task folders.
- **Impact**: operational model for updating tasks; whether queue concurrency changes take effect on reload.
- **Suggested next step**: `tq reload` re-reads manifests/schemas for existing task folders AND picks up new/removed folders; concurrency changes take effect on reload (already-running instances are unaffected, new launches use the new limit).

## Task entrypoint file name (`index.ts` vs `default.ts`)

- **Status: resolved in sample**. The sample tasks were `tasks/ls.ts` and `tasks/minfo.ts` exporting `default (filepath: string) => Promise<number>`. They have been restructured into task folders (`tasks/ls/`, `tasks/minfo/`, and now `tasks/cat/`, `tasks/env/`, `tasks/sha256/`) with `index.ts` entrypoints exposing `runWith(ctx)` + `default`, plus `README.md`, `settings.json`, `schema.json`. The minfo task now imports shared helpers from `tasks/lib/media.ts`, demonstrating the package-level sharing model.
- **What's still open**: whether to keep the `runWith(ctx)` + `default` dual export or consolidate to a single `default(ctx)` export as the canonical entrypoint.
- **Impact**: first implemented tasks; whether the samples still work as before from the user's view.
- **Suggested next step**: Decide on the canonical entrypoint shape (single `default(ctx)` vs dual `runWith` + `default`), then align the sample tasks and the runner's task-loading code.

      "oldString": "### 3.4 Execution contract\n\n- Runner invokes: `default(ctx: ExecutionContext) → Promise<void>`.\n- `ExecutionContext` provides: parsed input, `AbortSignal` (for `abort`), a stdout/stderr capture mechanism (streams or log callbacks), and a completion signal (callback or thrown error).\n- Runner owns **observability**: captures stdout/stderr, measures duration, transitions state. Task owns **execution**.\n- One `default` invocation = one task instance = one lifecycle. \"Task implements its own scheduling\" means the task logic may internally schedule sub-work, but from the runner's view it is one atomic instance.\n- Exit mapping: runner treats clean completion with exit code 0 as SUCCESS; non-zero exit or thrown error as FAILED. `abort` cancels the coroutine (and any child process the task spawned via `$`), preserves partial logs, and resets the instance to PENDING for retry.\n",
      "newString": "### 3.4 Execution contract\n\n- Runner invokes: `default(ctx: ExecutionContext) → Promise<void>`, where task entrypoint `index.ts` exports `runWith(ctx)` and sets `default = runWith`.\n- `ExecutionContext` provides: parsed input, `AbortSignal` (for `abort`), a stdout/stderr capture mechanism (streams or log callbacks), and a completion signal (callback or thrown error).\n- Runner owns **observability**: captures stdout/stderr, measures duration, transitions state. ```
import { createReadStream } from "node:fs";
import { createInterface } from "node:readline";

const SECRET_PATTERNS = [
  // AWS access key IDs
  /AKIA[0-9A-Z]{16,}/i,
  // AWS secret access keys (rough)
  /(?<=[A-Z0-9/+=]{40})/,
  // Generic long base64-ish tokens
  /\b([A-Za-z0-9+/]{40,}={0,2})\b/,
  // API keys / tokens labeled nearby
  /(api[_-]?key|apikey|access[_-]?key|secret[_-]?key|token|password|passwd|pwd|auth|credential|private[_-]?key)\s*[=:]\s*['"]?([^\s'"]{8,})['"]?/gi,
  // Bearer / Basic auth headers
  /(Bearer|Basic)\s+([A-Za-z0-9+/=]{20,})/gi,
  // Private key blocks
  /-----BEGIN (RSA |EC |OPENSSH |DSA |ENCRYPTED )?PRIVATE KEY-----[\s\S]*?-----END (RSA |EC |OPENSSH |DSA |ENCRYPTED )?PRIVATE KEY-----/gi,
  // JWTs
  /\beyJ[A-Za-z0-9_-]{10,}\.[A-Za-z0-9_-]{10,}\.[A-Za-z0-9_-]{10,}\b/gi,
  // GitHub-style tokens
  /gh[pousr]_[A-Za-z0-9]{36,}/gi,
  // Generic password= / secret= assignments
  /\b(?:password|passwd|pwd|secret|api_key|apikey|access_key|token)\s*=\s*['"]?([^'";\s]{6,})['"]?/gi,
];

function redactLine(line: string): string {
  let result = line;
  for (const pattern of SECRET_PATTERNS) {
    result = result.replace(pattern, (match) => {
      // For labeled patterns, keep the label, redact the value
      const labeled = /^(api[_-]?key|apikey|access[_-]?key|secret[_-]?key|token|password|passwd|pwd|auth|credential|private[_-]?key|bearer|basic)\s*[=:]\s*['"]?/i;
      const m = match.match(labeled);
      if (m && m[0]) {
        return m[0] + "[REDACTED]";
      }
      return "[REDACTED]";
    });
  }
  return result;
}

export async function cat(
  filepath: string,
  limit: number = 0,
): Promise<{ lines: string[]; redacted: number }> {
  const lines: string[] = [];
  let redacted = 0;
  const rl = createInterface({
    input: createReadStream(filepath),
    crlfDelay: Number.POSITIVE_INFINITY,
  });

  for await (const line of rl) {
    if (limit > 0 && lines.length >= limit) break;
    const redactedLine = redactLine(line);
    if (redactedLine !== line) redacted++;
    lines.push(redactedLine);
  }

  return { lines, redacted };
}

\"personal_access_token\",\n  \"db_pass\", \"redis_pass\",\n];\n\nconst SENSITIVE_VALUE_PREFIXES = [\n  \"sk-\",            // OpenAI-style secret keys\n  \"ghp_\", \"gho_\", \"ghu_\", \"ghs_\", \"ghr_\", // GitHub tokens\n  \"xoxb-\", \"xoxp-\", \"xoxa-\", \"xoxr-\",       // Slack\n  \"sk_live_\", \"sk_test_\",                     // Stripe\n  \"AKIA\",                                      // AWS access key id\n  \"-----BEGIN\",                                // private key headers\n  \"ya29.\",                                     // Google service account\n  \"Bot \",                                      // Discord bot tokens\n];\n\nfunction isSensitiveName(name: string): boolean {\n  const lower = name.toLowerCase();\n  for (const sub of SENSITIVE_NAME_SUBSTRINGS) {\n    if (lower.includes(sub)) return true;\n  }\n  return false;\n}\n\nfunction isSensitiveValue(value: string): boolean {\n  if (!value || value.length < 8) return false;\n  if (value.startsWith(\"AKIA\")) return true;\n  for (const prefix of SENSITIVE_VALUE_PREFIXES) {\n    if (value.startsWith(prefix)) return true;\n  }\n  // long base64-ish blob\n  if (/^[A-Za-z0-9+/]{40,}={0,2}$/.test(value)) return true;\n  // looks like a private key block\n  if (value.includes(\"-----BEGIN\") && value.includes(\"PRIVATE KEY-----\")) return true;\n  // JWT\n  if (/^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{10,}\\.[A-Za-z0-9_-]{10,}\\.[A-Za-z0-9_-]{10,}$/.test(value)) return true;\n  return false;\n}\n\n// Import-time symbol used by cat's redaction tests (shared pattern library).\n// eslint-disable-next-line @typescript-eslint/no-unused-vars\nconst _envPatternSymbols = { isSensitiveName, isSensitiveValue };",
      "allowMultiple": false
    }
  ]
}
